## Ritmo
* **Found in `VisionSense`**: - [LookTo|**Ritmo**] Head look like breathing rhythm if swimming front crawl
* **Found in `Strategy`**: - **Ritmo**, Passag, Kontakt
* **Found in `Ritmo.md`**: - Etymology(**Ritmo**)
* **Found in `Ritmo.md`**: - How **Ritmo** Interacts with Pulse and Beat
* **Found in `Ritmo.md`**: - **Ritmo** Animates the Pulse: The pulse provides the steady grid, while **Ritmo** provides the variety. **Ritmo** can mimic the pulse exactly, or it can move faster, slower, or stop entirely while the pulse continues silently underneath.**Ritmo**
* **Found in `Ritmo.md`**: - Divides the Beat: Beats are equal units of time. **Ritmo** breaks these units into smaller pieces (like eighth notes or sixteenth notes) or stretches across multiple beats (like half notes or whole notes).
* **Found in `Ritmo.md`**: - **Ritmo** Creates Syncopation: When a **Ritmo** deliberately places accents between the steady beats rather than directly on them, it creates syncopation. This friction between the unexpected **Ritmo** and the expected pulse is what makes music swing or feel groovy.

## Gravity
* **Found in `Axiom`**: - Axiom: **Gravity**
* **Found in `Klimon`**: - A dune isn't the same as a flat land which is inclined. The soil is stacked. **Gravity** isn't perpendicular to surface of a slope, which makes the surficial material act differently than a flat surface.

* **Found in `Drop`**: - **Gravity**
* **Found in `Pugno`**: - You never need to punch, instead just place at Initial Position and Lean. Use **Gravity** instead of Muscle to move the soil and Kompakt

* **Found in `Forces`**: - Local Mass using **Gravity** to add energy to an AKTION
* **Found in `Acceleration`**: - **Gravity**
* **Found in `Triceps`**: - [Drop|Arm] results in [Arm|Extension] so does [Extend|Elbow]. Drop uses **Gravity** to bring Elbow to Extended Position while Extend brings Elbow to extended position using Muscle.Triceps

## Beat
* **Found in `Fenomenology`**: - **Beat**s of FENOMs
* **Found in `Ritmo.md`**: - Strong**Beat** Weak**Beat**
* **Found in `Ritmo.md`**: - Stressed**Beat** Unstressed**Beat**
* **Found in `Ritmo.md`**: - Listening to Music while planting can give planters an artificial **Beat** to follow
* **Found in `Ritmo.md`**: - How Ritmo Interacts with Pulse and **Beat**
* **Found in `Ritmo.md`**: - Divides the **Beat**: **Beat**s are equal units of time. Ritmo breaks these units into smaller pieces (like eighth notes or sixteenth notes) or stretches across multiple beats (like half notes or whole notes).
* **Found in `Pulse`**: - Differentiate(Pulse,**Beat**)
* **Found in `Syncopation`**: - Stepping is the Strong**Beat**. [Spade.Lift] for [Spade.Slam] slightly Off**Beat**

## Pulse
* **Found in `Beat`**: - Differentiate(**Pulse**,Beat)
* **Found in `Ritmo.md`**: - How Ritmo Interacts with **Pulse** and Beat
* **Found in `Ritmo.md`**: - Ritmo Animates the **Pulse**: The pulse provides the steady grid, while Ritmo provides the variety. Ritmo can mimic the pulse exactly, or it can move faster, slower, or stop entirely while the pulse continues silently underneath.Ritmo

## Syncopation
* **Found in `Ritmo.md`**: - Ritmo Creates **Syncopation**: When a Ritmo deliberately places accents between the steady beats rather than directly on them, it creates syncopation. This friction between the unexpected Ritmo and the expected pulse is what makes music swing or feel groovy.
